Applied to all 3 portals.\n173\t\n174\t---\n175\t\n176\t## OBI Voice + Chat Fixes — 2026-05-29\n177\t\n178\t### Root Cause: Serial context fetches killed latency\n179\t\n180\t`handleChat()` in `index.js` had **7 sequential `await` calls** before the DeepSeek call:\n181\tLedger DO → Session DO → Weakness DO → Pet DO → `buildStructuredContext()` (4 more DOs) → Soul logger HTTP → KV reads.\n182\tEach could be 200-500ms. Total: up to 4-8 seconds before the LLM even started.\n183\t\n184\t**Fix:** Collapsed all 7 into a single `Promise.allSettled([...])` round — all fire simultaneously. Time = slowest single fetch, not the sum.\n185\t\n186\tAlso: `rosterStub.fetch()` and `updateStudentMemory()` were both `await`ed AFTER DeepSeek returned. Made fire-and-forget (`.catch(() => {})`).\n187\t\n188\t### Root Cause: Bilingual mode silently dropped\n189\t\n190\t`capContextInjection()` has a 1200-char hard cap. `vietnameseContext` was passed as the 8th argument — ledger/session/weakness filled the budget first, Vietnamese instruction was silently cut every time.\n191\t\n192\t**Fix:** `vietnameseContext` and `teachingFocusContext` now passed FIRST in `capContextInjection` — guaranteed never truncated.\n193\t\n194\t### Root Cause: teachingFocus never reached the server\n195\t\n196\t`handleChat()` only read `lang:<id>` from KV, never the student profile. `teachingFocus` (\"Speak VERY slowly, short sentences...\") was only available client-side, but the server-side prompt REPLACES the client's system prompt.\n197\t\n198\t**Fix:** `handleChat()` now reads `student_profile_<id>` from KV in parallel with `lang:<id>`, extracts `teachingFocus`, injects as `TEACHER INSTRUCTION` block.\n199\t\n200\t### Root Cause: isVague() matched \"anything\" as substring"

Three simple ways to keep the momentum going — no pressure, no homework:</p>\n580\t      <ul class=\"action-list\">\n581\t        <li><strong>Ask about his creations:</strong> When he builds something with Legos or toys, ask \"What's the problem?\" and \"How do we solve it?\" — the same way we do in class.</li>\n582\t        <li><strong>Celebrate the attempts:</strong> If he uses an English word or phrase at home, praise the confidence — not the grammar. Correction comes later; right now confidence is everything.</li>\n583\t        <li><strong>Follow his interests:</strong> Talk about the helicopter on Shopee. Ask what kind of shark he would be. When the topic is his choice, the English flows by itself.</li>\n584\t      </ul>\n585\t    </section>\n586\t\n587\t    <section class=\"card teacher-note\">\n588\t      <h2>WHAT'S NEXT</h2>\n589\t      <p>Our next sessions will focus on building Minh's storytelling into longer, more structured narratives — keeping the game-based approach that clearly works best for him.
